(This is\n40\t *     the original regression class.)\n41\t *   - An init-script SQL file was deleted without an equivalent\n42\t *     migration → either restore the script OR write a migration that\n43\t *     creates the same tables idempotently.\n44\t *   - A new entity was added without a corresponding CREATE TABLE\n45\t *     migration AND without an init-script entry → add the migration.\n46\t *   - The init scripts assume a privilege the bootstrap POSTGRES_USER\n47\t *     does not have on a fresh container → fix the script to use\n48\t *     a less-privileged path or supply additional bootstrap material.\n49\t *\n50\t * # Why testcontainers (not the shared TestDatabase pool)\n51\t *\n52\t * The other e2e/integration specs assert state of an *already-running*\n53\t * database that the dev/CI infra brought up — they're checking\n54\t * application-level behaviour. This test asserts the bootstrap ITSELF\n55\t * works on an EMPTY volume. It MUST start with no PGDATA, run the init\n56\t * scripts, then run the migration chain. testcontainers is the only\n57\t * way to get that hermetic guarantee inside a Jest worker.\n58\t *\n59\t * # Runtime\n60\t *\n61\t * ~3-5 minutes on a cold runner: image pull + init-script execution +\n62\t * 100+ migrations across 13 services. CI gate runs only on PRs that\n63\t * touch `apps/*\\/src/**\\/migrations/**` or\n64\t * `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/**` (see CI workflow\n65\t * `.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml` integration; if the gate\n66\t * is not yet wired, `npm run test:bootstrap` is the local entry point).\n67\t */\n68\t\n69\timport { readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from 'fs';\n70\timport { createRequire } from 'module';\n71\timport { join, resolve } from 'path';\n72\t\n73\timport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n74\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n75\timport { GenericContainer, type StartedTestContainer, Wait } from 'testcontainers';\n76\timport { DataSource, type MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n77\t\n78\t// ADR-031 Platform Bootstrap Atom — runs the schema/role/extension/function/\n79\t// shared-table DDL contract that init-scripts USED to own pre-cutover. The\n80\t// init-scripts directory copied below now contains only `01-init-databases.sql`\n81\t// (initdb-only DB GRANTs). Schemas, roles, functions, and shared.* tables\n82\t// are created by the call to runPlatformBootstrap() below before any\n83\t// per-service migration loop runs.\n84\timport {\n85\t  runPlatformBootstrap,\n86\t  resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir,\n87\t} from '../platform-bootstrap.service';\n88\timport { ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority } from '../tenant-sensor-continuous-aggregate-authority';\n89\t\n90\t// Constructor type for the @Entity / migration classes this spec loads\n91\t// dynamically. TypeORM's DataSource `entities`/`migrations` options accept\n92\t// `Function`, but `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-function-type` (correctly)\n93\t// rejects the bare `Function` type; a precise construct signature is both\n94\t// assignable to TypeORM's `Function` slot AND keeps the collected values\n95\t// typed as classes rather than arbitrary callables.\n96\ttype EntityClass = new (...args: never[]) => object;\n97\t\n98\t// Synchronous module loader bound to this spec's location. `import`/`await\n99\t// import()` cannot be used here: loadMigrationClassesFromDir is invoked inside\n100\t// `it.each(...)` arguments that Jest evaluates synchronously at collection\n101\t// time, so the load must be synchronous. `createRequire(__filename)` resolves\n102\t// migration classes and the cross-package schema-drift-validator through\n103\t// ts-jest at runtime, so the static type-check graph never reaches across\n104\t// project rootDirs — the same dynamic-load pattern the db-migrate\n105\t// platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts relies on.\n106\tconst requireModule = createRequire(__filename);\n107\t\n108\t// Structured logger for the (rare) entity-load skip path — `console.*` is\n109\t// banned platform-wide; the NestJS Logger keeps the diagnostic on the same\n110\t// structured-logging path the runtime services use.\n111\tconst logger = new Logger('bootstrap-from-scratch');\n112\t\n113\t// Local narrowing helper. db-migrate has @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion\n114\t// ENABLED (unlike the e2e project, whose assertDefined SSoT this replaces), so a\n115\t// bare `!` is banned here. assertDefined narrows `T | undefined` to `T` and fails\n116\t// loudly at the assertion site instead of surfacing as a cryptic\n117\t// \"cannot read properties of undefined\" further downstream.\n118\tfunction assertDefined<T>(value: T | null | undefined, message?: string): T {\n119\t  if (value === null || value === undefined) {\n120\t    throw new Error(message ?? 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');\n121\t  }\n122\t  return value;\n123\t}\n124\t\n125\t// Dynamic load of the backend-common schema-drift-validator. Importing\n126\t// schema-drift-validator.service.ts directly via `import { ... }` would pull a\n127\t// file from another Nx project into db-migrate's type-check graph. ts-jest\n128\t// resolves this lazily at runtime (same pattern the migration loader uses for\n129\t// migration classes), so the validator's stable runtime contract is exercised\n130\t// without a static cross-project import. From this spec's location\n131\t// (apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/) the repo root is four levels up.\n132\ttype SchemaDriftValidatorFactory = (\n133\t  serviceName: string,\n134\t) => new (ds: DataSource, cs: ConfigService) => { onApplicationBootstrap(): Promise<void> };\n135\tfunction loadDriftValidatorFactory(): SchemaDriftValidatorFactory {\n136\t  const mod = requireModule(\n137\t    '../../../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-drift-validator.service',\n138\t  ) as {\n139\t    createSchemaDriftValidator: SchemaDriftValidatorFactory;\n140\t  };\n141\t  return mod.createSchemaDriftValidator;\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\t// The same image+digest the production stack uses — see\n145\t// docker-compose.infra.yml line 12. Pinning by digest guarantees the\n146\t// init-script behaviour we're proving here matches what runs in\n147\t// production. Bumping the digest is a deliberate review surface.\n148\tconst POSTGRES_IMAGE =\n149\t  'timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7';\n150\t\n151\tconst DATABASE_NAME = 'aquaculture';\n152\tconst DATABASE_USER = 'aquaculture';\n153\tconst DATABASE_PASSWORD = 'aquaculture-test';\n154\tconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\n155\tconst SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS = [\n156\t  'AUTH_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n157\t  'FARM_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n158\t  'SENSOR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n159\t  'BILLING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n160\t  'HR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n161\t  'ALERT_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n162\t  'ADMIN_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n163\t  'GATEWAY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n164\t  'NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n165\t  'HYDROPONICS_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n166\t  'AI_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n167\t  'MESSAGING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n168\t  'OBSERVABILITY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n169\t  'EVENT_STORE_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n170\t  'CONFIG_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n171\t] as const;\n172\t\n173\t// Repo root, derived from this file's location:\n174\t//   apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts -> ../../../..\n175\t// (mirrors how the sibling platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts resolves it).\n176\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..');\n177\tconst INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR = join(REPO_ROOT, 'infrastructure', 'docker', 'init-scripts');\n178\t\n179\tasync function runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n180\t  const previous = process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV];\n181\t  process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = '1';\n182\t  try {\n183\t    return await operation();\n184\t  } finally {\n185\t    if (previous === undefined) {\n186\t      Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env, DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV);\n187\t    } else {\n188\t      process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = previous;\n189\t    }\n190\t  }\n191\t}\n192\t\n193\t/**\n194\t * Per-service migration manifest.\n195\t *\n196\t * Each entry maps a service to:\n197\t *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n198\t *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n199\t *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n200\t *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n201\t *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n202\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n203\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n204\t *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n205\t *     when adding a service.\n206\t *\n207\t * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n208\t * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n209\t * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n210\t * else is alphabetical.\n211\t */\n212\tinterface ServiceManifest {\n213\t  name: string;\n214\t  schema: string;\n215\t  migrationsDir: string;\n216\t  /**\n217\t   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n218\t   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n219\t   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n220\t   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n221\t   * exists in the DB. Services without entities (gateway-api) leave it\n222\t   * unset.\n223\t   */\n224\t  entitiesGlob?: string;\n225\t}\n226\t\n227\tconst SERVICES: ServiceManifest[] = [\n228\t  // auth FIRST — every other service's FKs reference auth.tenants.\n229\t  {\n230\t    name: 'auth-service',\n231\t    schema: 'auth',\n232\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations',\n233\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/auth-service/src',\n234\t  },\n235\t  // Then the rest, ordered to minimize cross-schema FK ordering hazards.\n236\t  {\n237\t    name: 'farm-service',\n238\t    schema: 'farm',\n239\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations',\n240\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/farm-service/src',\n241\t  },\n242\t  {\n243\t    name: 'sensor-service',\n244\t    schema: 'sensor',\n245\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations',\n246\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/sensor-service/src',\n247\t  },\n248\t  {\n249\t    name: 'hr-service',\n250\t    schema: 'hr',\n251\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations',\n252\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hr-service/src',\n253\t  },\n254\t  {\n255\t    name: 'messaging-service',\n256\t    schema: 'messaging',\n257\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations',\n258\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/messaging-service/src',\n259\t  },\n260\t  {\n261\t    name: 'alert-engine',\n262\t    schema: 'alert',\n263\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations',\n264\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/alert-engine/src',\n265\t  },\n266\t  {\n267\t    name: 'billing-service',\n268\t    schema: 'billing',\n269\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/billing-service/src/database/migrations',\n270\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/billing-service/src',\n271\t  },\n272\t  {\n273\t    name: 'notification-service',\n274\t    schema: 'notification',\n275\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/notification-service/src/database/migrations',\n276\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/notification-service/src',\n277\t  },\n278\t  {\n279\t    name: 'observability-service',\n280\t    schema: 'observability',\n281\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/observability-service/src/database/migrations',\n282\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/observability-service/src',\n283\t  },\n284\t  {\n285\t    name: 'admin-api-service',\n286\t    schema: 'admin',\n287\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations',\n288\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/admin-api-service/src',\n289\t  },\n290\t  {\n291\t    name: 'event-store-service',\n292\t    schema: 'event_store',\n293\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/event-store-service/src/migrations',\n294\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/event-store-service/src',\n295\t  },\n296\t  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — close the manifest gap. The 4 services\n297\t  // below were absent from the original 11-service manifest, so the\n298\t  // bootstrap test passed even when their migration chains were empty\n299\t  // or their entity-surface drifted from the on-disk schema. Adding\n300\t  // them here closes the regression class:\n301\t  //\n302\t  //   hydroponics-service / ai-service — schema-per-tenant services\n303\t  //     with their own baseline migrations + entity surface.\n304\t  //   config-service — platform-level service. Migrations dir is\n305\t  //     intentionally empty (.gitkeep only) — the entity-surface\n306\t  //     check still runs, the migration runner is a no-op.\n307\t  //   gateway-api — pure HTTP fronting, owns NO entities and NO\n308\t  //     migrations. Listed in the manifest so the schema-ownership\n309\t  //     audit (Part B B.4) can assert the `gateway` schema exists\n310\t  //     and is owned by `gateway_service`. `entitiesGlob` is unset\n311\t  //     → entity-surface check skips it (correct: nothing to check).\n312\t  {\n313\t    name: 'hydroponics-service',\n314\t    schema: 'hydroponics',\n315\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/hydroponics-service/src/database/migrations',\n316\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hydroponics-service/src',\n317\t  },\n318\t  {\n319\t    name: 'ai-service',\n320\t    schema: 'ai',\n321\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/ai-service/src/database/migrations',\n322\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/ai-service/src',\n323\t  },\n324\t  {\n325\t    name: 'config-service',\n326\t    schema: 'config',\n327\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/config-service/src/database/migrations',\n328\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/config-service/src',\n329\t  },\n330\t  {\n331\t    name: 'gateway-api',\n332\t    schema: 'gateway',\n333\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/gateway-api/src/database/migrations',\n334\t    // gateway-api owns no entities — `entitiesGlob` intentionally unset.\n335\t  },\n336\t];\n337\t\n338\t/**\n339\t * Tables expected to be registered as TimescaleDB hypertables in the SOURCE\n340\t * schemas after the full bootstrap pipeline runs.\n341\t *\n342\t * `sensor_readings` is the retired per-reading store: nothing reads or writes\n343\t * it any more (SENSOR-HIGH-085 replaced it with an as-of projection over\n344\t * sensor_metrics), but Baseline still creates it and it still holds the\n345\t * historical rows, so its hypertable registration remains part of the bootstrap\n346\t * contract until F-085-DROP removes the table outright.\n347\t *\n348\t * `sensor_metrics` is the per-tenant channel-keyed telemetry hypertable. This\n349\t * list only covers the `sensor` source copy — the per-tenant copies come from\n350\t * replaying 1815000000000-CreateTenantSensorMetricsHypertable into each tenant\n351\t * schema, which this spec does not exercise because it never provisions a\n352\t * tenant. That gap is not cosmetic: DATA-CRITICAL-010 shows a fresh tenant\n353\t * provision aborts before reaching 1815 at all.\n354\t */\n355\tconst EXPECTED_HYPERTABLES: Array<{ schema: string; table: string }> = [\n356\t  { schema: 'sensor', table: 'sensor_readings' },\n357\t  { schema: 'sensor', table: 'sensor_metrics' },\n358\t];\n359\t\n360\t/**\n361\t * Schemas whose owning services run in schema-per-tenant mode (ADR-011 +\n362\t * libs/backend-common/.../tenant-aware-schemas.ts SSoT). Every such\n363\t * source schema MUST have RLS policies registered against its tables —\n364\t * otherwise the tenant_<uuid> clones inherit ZERO policies via\n365\t * CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL and a row-leakage hole opens.\n366\t *\n367\t * Asserted by Part A's RLS-policy probe (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb)\n368\t * and Part B B.5's TENANT_SCOPED audit. Both consumers import this\n369\t * constant via the SERVICES manifest.\n370\t */\n371\tconst RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n372\t  'farm',\n373\t  'sensor',\n374\t  'hr',\n375\t  'messaging',\n376\t  'hydroponics',\n377\t  'ai',\n378\t  'alert',\n379\t]);\n380\t\n381\t/**\n382\t * Schemas that MUST exist after the init scripts have run, BEFORE any\n383\t * TypeORM migration touches the DB. These are created exclusively by\n384\t * `00-init-schemas.sh` in the init-scripts bind mount.\n385\t */\n386\tconst REQUIRED_SCHEMAS = [\n387\t  'auth',\n388\t  'farm',\n389\t  'sensor',\n390\t  'hr',\n391\t  'messaging',\n392\t  'hydroponics',\n393\t  'alert',\n394\t  'billing',\n395\t  'notification',\n396\t  'ai',\n397\t  'admin',\n398\t  'observability',\n399\t  'event_store',\n400\t  'shared',\n401\t  'gateway',\n402\t];\n403\t\n404\t/**\n405\t * Critical columns on `auth.users` that the previous regression class\n406\t * silently dropped. Each one was added by `1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema`\n407\t * after the squash damage was identified — proving they exist after a\n408\t * fresh-volume init is the exact failure mode this test guards.\n409\t */\n410\tconst CRITICAL_AUTH_USERS_COLUMNS = [\n411\t  'mfaLockedUntil',\n412\t  'mfaRecoveryCodes',\n413\t  'mfaFailedAttempts',\n414\t  'accessType',\n415\t  'notificationPreferences',\n416\t];\n417\t\n418\t/**\n419\t * Baseline tables in `auth` schema that were lost in the squash and\n420\t * restored by `1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema`. Asserting all 12 exist\n421\t * is the most direct proof the migration chain rebuilt the auth surface.\n422\t */\n423\tconst AUTH_BASELINE_TABLES = [\n424\t  'refresh_tokens',\n425\t  'webauthn_credentials',\n426\t  'user_module_assignments',\n427\t  'mobile_user_settings',\n428\t  'modules',\n429\t  'announcements',\n430\t  'announcement_acknowledgments',\n431\t  'message_threads',\n432\t  'messages',\n433\t  'support_tickets',\n434\t  'ticket_comments',\n435\t  'audit_logs',\n436\t];\n437\t\n438\t/**\n439\t * Dynamically load every migration class from a directory.\n440\t *\n441\t * Strategy: iterate the directory, filter to `<13-digit>-<Name>.ts` files\n442\t * (the codebase's enforced filename convention — see\n443\t * .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml step \"Validate migration\n444\t * filename...\"), require each, and pull the default-or-named export\n445\t * whose name ends with the timestamp. Migration classes follow the\n446\t * convention `<Name><timestamp>` (e.g. `CreateInitialSchema1700000000000`).\n447\t */\n448\tfunction loadMigrationClassesFromDir(absDir: string): Array<new () => MigrationInterface> {\n449\t  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — services that own a directory but ship no\n450\t  // migrations yet (config-service: .gitkeep only; gateway-api:\n451\t  // directory absent entirely) are valid manifest entries; return an\n452\t  // empty class list so the per-service runMigrations() call is a no-op\n453\t  // rather than a hard failure.\n454\t  if (!existsSync(absDir)) {\n455\t    return [];\n456\t  }\n457\t\n458\t  let entries: string[];\n459\t  try {\n460\t    entries = readdirSync(absDir);\n461\t  } catch (err) {\n462\t    throw new Error(\n463\t      `Migration directory ${absDir} could not be read: ${(err as Error).message}. ` +\n464\t        `Update SERVICES manifest if the path moved.`,\n465\t    );\n466\t  }\n467\t\n468\t  const migrationFiles = entries\n469\t    .filter((f) => /^[0-9]{13}-[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*\\.ts$/.test(f))\n470\t    .filter((f) => !f.endsWith('.spec.ts') && !f.endsWith('.test.ts'))\n471\t    .sort(); // lexical sort = chronological because timestamps are zero-padded.\n472\t\n473\t  const classes: Array<new () => MigrationInterface> = [];\n474\t  for (const file of migrationFiles) {\n475\t    const fullPath = join(absDir, file);\n476\t    if (!statSync(fullPath).isFile()) continue;\n477\t\n478\t    // ts-jest transforms .ts files when loaded through requireModule here\n479\t    // because this spec runs under the ts-jest transform configured in\n480\t    // e2e/jest.config.ts. Each migration's `export class <Name>` becomes\n481\t    // a property on the loaded module object.\n482\t    const mod = requireModule(fullPath) as Record<string, unknown>;\n483\t    const candidate = Object.values(mod).find(\n484\t      (v): v is new () => MigrationInterface =>\n485\t        typeof v === 'function' && /^[A-Z]/.test(v.name) && /\\d{13}$/.test(v.name),\n486\t    );\n487\t    if (!candidate) {\n488\t      throw new Error(\n489\t        `Migration file ${fullPath} did not export a class whose name matches ` +\n490\t          `<PascalCaseName><13-digit-timestamp>. Filename + class-name convention ` +\n491\t          `is enforced by .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml.`,\n492\t      );\n493\t    }\n494\t    classes.push(candidate);\n495\t  }\n496\t  return classes;\n497\t}\n498\t\n499\t/**\n500\t * Discover every `*.entity.ts` file under a service's source root.\n501\t *\n502\t * Used by the entity-surface coverage matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb).\n503\t * Recurses through subdirectories because services scatter entities across\n504\t * `src/<feature>/entities/`, `src/<feature>/`, and the legacy\n505\t * `src/setup/entities/` location.\n506\t */\n507\tfunction findEntityFiles(absRoot: string): string[] {\n508\t  const out: string[] = [];\n509\t  if (!existsSync(absRoot)) return out;\n510\t  const stack: string[] = [absRoot];\n511\t  while (stack.length > 0) {\n512\t    // The `while (stack.length > 0)` guard makes `pop()` defined here;\n513\t    // assertDefined narrows it without a banned non-null assertion.\n514\t    const current = assertDefined(stack.pop());\n515\t    let entries: string[];\n516\t    try {\n517\t      entries = readdirSync(current);\n518\t    } catch {\n519\t      continue;\n520\t    }\n521\t    for (const name of entries) {\n522\t      // Skip migration directories — files there are CREATE TABLE\n523\t      // statements, not entity declarations. Skip __tests__ to avoid\n524\t      // pulling spec doubles into the metadata graph.\n525\t      if (name === 'migrations' || name === '__tests__' || name === 'node_modules') {\n526\t        continue;\n527\t      }\n528\t      const full = join(current, name);\n529\t      let st;\n530\t      try {\n531\t        st = statSync(full);\n532\t      } catch {\n533\t        continue;\n534\t      }\n535\t      if (st.isDirectory()) {\n536\t        stack.push(full);\n537\t        continue;\n538\t      }\n539\t      if (st.isFile() && /\\.entity\\.ts$/.test(name)) {\n540\t        out.push(full);\n541\t      }\n542\t    }\n543\t  }\n544\t  return out;\n545\t}\n546\t\n547\t/**\n548\t * Load every `@Entity` class from a service's source root.\n549\t *\n550\t * Strategy: ts-jest transforms .ts files on load, so each entity file\n551\t * registers itself with TypeORM's getMetadataArgsStorage() side-effect.\n552\t * After loading all files we instantiate a metadata-only DataSource\n553\t * to materialize EntityMetadata graphs that the surface-matrix probes\n554\t * iterate over.\n555\t */\n556\tfunction loadEntityClasses(absRoot: string): EntityClass[] {\n557\t  const files = findEntityFiles(absRoot);\n558\t  const classes: EntityClass[] = [];\n559\t  const loadFailures: string[] = [];\n560\t  for (const file of files) {\n561\t    let mod: Record<string, unknown>;\n562\t    try {\n563\t      mod = requireModule(file) as Record<string, unknown>;\n564\t    } catch (err) {\n565\t      // A .entity.ts that fails to load is NEVER safe to skip. Because the\n566\t      // require throws, the module body never executes — so its @Entity\n567\t      // decorators do NOT register with getMetadataArgsStorage and the entity\n568\t      // is fully ABSENT from the metadata graph. Silently continuing would let\n569\t      // that entity's table pass the schema-drift matrix by omission: a\n570\t      // genuinely missing table/column would go undetected. Collect every\n571\t      // failure and fail the suite loudly (see throw below).\n572\t      const message = `${file}: ${(err as Error).message}`;\n573\t      loadFailures.push(message);\n574\t      logger.error(`could not load entity file ${message}`);\n575\t      continue;\n576\t    }\n577\t    for (const v of Object.values(mod)) {\n578\t      if (isEntityClass(v)) {\n579\t        classes.push(v);\n580\t      }\n581\t    }\n582\t  }\n583\t  if (loadFailures.length > 0) {\n584\t    throw new Error(\n585\t      `loadEntityClasses(${absRoot}) failed to load ${loadFailures.length} ` +\n586\t        `entity file(s). An un-loadable entity silently drops out of the ` +\n587\t        `schema-drift matrix, so this is a hard failure — fix the ` +\n588\t        `compile/load error(s):\\n${loadFailures.map((f) => `  - ${f}`).join('\\n')}`,\n589\t    );\n590\t  }\n591\t  return classes;\n592\t}\n593\t\n594\t/** Narrow an unknown module export to a PascalCase-named class constructor. */\n595\tfunction isEntityClass(value: unknown): value is EntityClass {\n596\t  return typeof value === 'function' && /^[A-Z]/.test(value.name);\n597\t}\n598\t\n599\tinterface SurfaceProbeResult {\n600\t  schema: string;\n601\t  table: string;\n602\t  drift: string[];\n603\t}\n604\t\n605\t/**\n606\t * Assert that every entity registered for a service has a matching\n607\t * physical table, columns, FKs, indexes, and (for tenant-scoped\n608\t * schemas) at least one tenant_<uuid> RLS-policy clone.\n609\t *\n610\t * This is the generic helper the mission spec calls\n611\t * `assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb`. Returns the per-entity drift list;\n612\t * the caller decides how to surface failures (one Jest case per drift\n613\t * gives the highest-signal CI output).\n614\t */\n615\tinterface PgConnInfo {\n616\t  host: string;\n617\t  port: number;\n618\t  username: string;\n619\t  password: string;\n620\t  database: string;\n621\t}\n622\t\n623\tasync function assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb(\n624\t  serviceName: string,\n625\t  entityClasses: ReadonlyArray<EntityClass>,\n626\t  conn: PgConnInfo,\n627\t): Promise<SurfaceProbeResult[]> {\n628\t  // Build a metadata-only DataSource so EntityMetadata graphs are\n629\t  // materialized for FK + index introspection. We cannot use the\n630\t  // service's migration DataSource because that one is configured\n631\t  // with `migrations: [...]` and no entities. A short-lived metadata\n632\t  // DataSource avoids re-running migrations.\n633\t  const metadataDs = new DataSource({\n634\t    type: 'postgres',\n635\t    host: conn.host,\n636\t    port: conn.port,\n637\t    username: conn.username,\n638\t    password: conn.password,\n639\t    database: conn.database,\n640\t    entities: [...entityClasses],\n641\t    synchronize: false,\n642\t    migrationsRun: false,\n643\t  });\n644\t  await metadataDs.initialize();\n645\t\n646\t  const results: SurfaceProbeResult[] = [];\n647\t  try {\n648\t    for (const meta of metadataDs.entityMetadatas) {\n649\t      // Resolve declared schema. Tenant-aware entities legitimately\n650\t      // omit `schema:` (the SchemaManagerService routes them at\n651\t      // provision time). For source-table existence we look in the\n652\t      // canonical service schema derived from the manifest.\n653\t      const declaredSchema =\n654\t        meta.schema ??\n655\t        // Fall back to the service's canonical schema — tenant-aware\n656\t        // entities use this path.\n657\t        SERVICES.find((s) => s.name === serviceName)?.schema ??\n658\t        'public';\n659\t\n660\t      const drift: string[] = [];\n661\t      const tableName = meta.tableName;\n662\t\n663\t      // (a) Table exists in declared OR routed schema. We accept the\n664\t      //     declared schema as primary; tenant_* schemas are a\n665\t      //     secondary residence and skipped here (Part C covers them).\n666\t      const tableRows: Array<{ schemaname: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n667\t        `SELECT schemaname FROM pg_tables\n668\t         WHERE tablename = $1 AND schemaname = $2`,\n669\t        [tableName, declaredSchema],\n670\t      );\n671\t      if (tableRows.length === 0) {\n672\t        drift.push(`table missing: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} not found in pg_tables`);\n673\t        results.push({ schema: declaredSchema, table: tableName, drift });\n674\t        continue;\n675\t      }\n676\t\n677\t      // (b) Column existence + data-type compatibility per @Column.\n678\t      const columnRows: Array<{ column_name: string; data_type: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n679\t        `SELECT column_name, data_type FROM information_schema.columns\n680\t           WHERE table_schema = $1 AND table_name = $2`,\n681\t        [declaredSchema, tableName],\n682\t      );\n683\t      const dbColumns = new Map(columnRows.map((r) => [r.column_name, r.data_type]));\n684\t      for (const col of meta.columns) {\n685\t        if (!dbColumns.has(col.databaseName)) {\n686\t          drift.push(\n687\t            `column missing: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName}.${col.databaseName} ` +\n688\t              `(entity declares but DB has no such column)`,\n689\t          );\n690\t        }\n691\t      }\n692\t\n693\t      // (c) FKs: every @ManyToOne relation has a pg_constraint row.\n694\t      //     We count FKs whose constrained relation matches the table;\n695\t      //     mismatched count is the drift signal (TypeORM emits one\n696\t      //     constraint per relation).\n697\t      const expectedFkCount = meta.relations.filter((r) => r.relationType === 'many-to-one').length;\n698\t      if (expectedFkCount > 0) {\n699\t        const fkRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n700\t          `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_constraint c\n701\t             JOIN pg_class t ON t.oid = c.conrelid\n702\t             JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = t.relnamespace\n703\t           WHERE c.contype = 'f'\n704\t             AND n.nspname = $1\n705\t             AND t.relname = $2`,\n706\t          [declaredSchema, tableName],\n707\t        );\n708\t        const actual = parseInt(fkRows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n709\t        if (actual < expectedFkCount) {\n710\t          drift.push(\n711\t            `fk drift: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} declares ${expectedFkCount} ` +\n712\t              `@ManyToOne relation(s) but pg_constraint has only ${actual} FK(s)`,\n713\t          );\n714\t        }\n715\t      }\n716\t\n717\t      // (d) Indexes: every @Index has a pg_indexes row. We do a\n718\t      //     count-only check because TypeORM-generated index names\n719\t      //     are unstable across versions; the drift signal is \"DB\n720\t      //     has fewer indexes than the entity declares\" — extra\n721\t      //     indexes are fine.\n722\t      const expectedIxCount = meta.indices.length;\n723\t      if (expectedIxCount > 0) {\n724\t        const ixRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n725\t          `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_indexes\n726\t           WHERE schemaname = $1 AND tablename = $2`,\n727\t          [declaredSchema, tableName],\n728\t        );\n729\t        const actual = parseInt(ixRows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n730\t        // The PK always has its own index — entity @Index list does\n731\t        // not include it. Accept actual >= expectedIxCount + 0 (PK\n732\t        // index counted, declared @Index entries each add one).\n733\t        if (actual < expectedIxCount) {\n734\t          drift.push(\n735\t            `index drift: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} declares ${expectedIxCount} ` +\n736\t              `@Index entries, pg_indexes has only ${actual} (excluding PK)`,\n737\t          );\n738\t        }\n739\t      }\n740\t\n741\t      // (e) For tenant-scoped schemas: at least one tenant_<uuid> clone\n742\t      //     must carry an RLS policy on the cloned table. The bootstrap\n743\t      //     test runs against an empty-tenant DB (no tenants\n744\t      //     provisioned), so we cannot fail on \"no clone exists\" — we\n745\t      //     only fail when a clone EXISTS and is missing RLS. Part C\n746\t      //     spec covers the active-clone shape with a real tenant.\n747\t      if (RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS.has(declaredSchema)) {\n748\t        const cloneRows: Array<{\n749\t          tablename: string;\n750\t          schemaname: string;\n751\t        }> = await metadataDs.query(\n752\t          `SELECT tablename, schemaname FROM pg_tables\n753\t           WHERE schemaname ~ '^tenant_[a-f0-9]{16}$'\n754\t             AND tablename = $1`,\n755\t          [tableName],\n756\t        );\n757\t        for (const cr of cloneRows) {\n758\t          const policyRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n759\t            `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_policies\n760\t             WHERE schemaname = $1 AND tablename = $2`,\n761\t            [cr.schemaname, cr.tablename],\n762\t          );\n763\t          if (parseInt(policyRows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10) === 0) {\n764\t            drift.push(\n765\t              `rls drift: tenant clone ${cr.schemaname}.${cr.tablename} has zero pg_policies rows`,\n766\t            );\n767\t          }\n768\t        }\n769\t      }\n770\t\n771\t      results.push({ schema: declaredSchema, table: tableName, drift });\n772\t    }\n773\t  } finally {\n774\t    await metadataDs.destroy().catch(() => {\n775\t      /* swallow — teardown */\n776\t    });\n777\t  }\n778\t  return results;\n779\t}\n780\t\n781\tdescribe('Bootstrap from scratch (fresh-volume init + full migration chain)', () => {\n782\t  let postgresContainer: StartedTestContainer;\n783\t  // Per-service DataSources, kept open across `beforeAll` so the migration\n784\t  // ledger queries in the assertion phase don't have to reopen connections.\n785\t  const dataSources = new Map<string, DataSource>();\n786\t\n787\t  beforeAll(async () => {\n788\t    for (const envName of SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS) {\n789\t      process.env[envName] ??= `${envName.toLowerCase()}_test`;\n790\t    }\n791\t\n792\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n793\t    // 1. Boot Postgres with init-scripts copied in.\n794\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n795\t    // The `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` directory is consumed by the\n796\t    // postgres image's entrypoint exactly once, when PGDATA is empty.\n797\t    // Testcontainers archives the live repo directory into the container, so\n798\t    // the test exercises the SAME scripts the production deploy runs without\n799\t    // depending on host-workspace traversal permissions. An explicit mode is\n800\t    // required because a restrictive checkout umask must not make the copied\n801\t    // init directory unreadable by the postgres image's entrypoint user.\n802\t    postgresContainer = await new GenericContainer(POSTGRES_IMAGE)\n803\t      .withEnvironment({\n804\t        POSTGRES_DB: DATABASE_NAME,\n805\t        POSTGRES_USER: DATABASE_USER,\n806\t        POSTGRES_PASSWORD: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n807\t        // The platform bootstrap atom refuses empty service-role password\n808\t        // envs. Forward deterministic dummy values into the container so\n809\t        // init scripts and Phase 0 exercise the same env contract as prod.\n810\t        ...Object.fromEntries(\n811\t          SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS.map((envName) => [\n812\t            envName,\n813\t            process.env[envName] ?? `${envName.toLowerCase()}_test`,\n814\t          ]),\n815\t        ),\n816\t      })\n817\t      .withCopyDirectoriesToContainer([\n818\t        {\n819\t          source: INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR,\n820\t          target: '/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d',\n821\t          mode: 0o755,\n822\t        },\n823\t      ])\n824\t      .withExposedPorts(5432)\n825\t      // Wait for `database system is ready to accept connections` AFTER\n826\t      // init scripts complete — the postgres entrypoint logs this message\n827\t      // when the second startup (post-init-scripts) finishes.\n828\t      .withWaitStrategy(Wait.forLogMessage(/database system is ready to accept connections/, 2))\n829\t      .withStartupTimeout(180_000)\n830\t      .start();\n831\t\n832\t    const host = postgresContainer.getHost();\n833\t    const port = postgresContainer.getMappedPort(5432);\n834\t\n835\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n836\t    // 1.5. Run the ADR-031 platform-bootstrap atom.\n837\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n838\t    // Pre-ADR-031, init-scripts ran the entire platform DDL contract on\n839\t    // initdb. Post-ADR-031, init-scripts hold only DB-level GRANTs;\n840\t    // schemas/roles/extensions/functions/shared.* tables come from this\n841\t    // atom which is identical to what aqua-db-migrate Phase 0 runs in\n842\t    // production. Calling it here from the test keeps the\n843\t    // bootstrap-from-scratch fixture aligned with the live deploy path.\n844\t    await runPlatformBootstrap({\n845\t      database: {\n846\t        host,\n847\t        port,\n848\t        username: DATABASE_USER,\n849\t        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n850\t        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n851\t        ssl: false,\n852\t      },\n853\t      sqlDir: resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir(REPO_ROOT),\n854\t      log: () => undefined, // silent — Jest captures stdout per spec\n855\t      lockTimeoutSeconds: 60,\n856\t    });\n857\t\n858\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n859\t    // 2. Run every service's migration chain in dependency order.\n860\t    // -----------------------------------------------------------------\n861\t    for (const svc of SERVICES) {\n862\t      const absDir = join(REPO_ROOT, svc.migrationsDir);\n863\t      const migrationClasses = loadMigrationClassesFromDir(absDir);\n864\t\n865\t      // Load entity classes for services that declare an entitiesGlob.\n866\t      // Required for migrations that introspect connection.entityMetadatas\n867\t      // — notably hr-service's SyncHrEntitiesToDb1786800000000 which\n868\t      // derives its DDL emit + entity-default registry from the entity\n869\t      // surface. Other services tolerate the empty-entities path (their\n870\t      // migrations are pure DDL). Services without entitiesGlob\n871\t      // (gateway-api) keep an empty list. Pairs with the\n872\t      // ALTER-COLUMN-TYPE default-recovery transform inside\n873\t      // SyncHrEntitiesToDb that prevents the \"default for column ...\n874\t      // cannot be cast automatically to type ..._enum\" failure mode on\n875\t      // fresh-volume bootstraps.\n876\t      const entityClasses: EntityClass[] = svc.entitiesGlob\n877\t        ? loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, svc.entitiesGlob))\n878\t        : [];\n879\t\n880\t      const ds = new DataSource({\n881\t        type: 'postgres',\n882\t        host,\n883\t        port,\n884\t        username: DATABASE_USER,\n885\t        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n886\t        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n887\t        schema: svc.schema,\n888\t        migrations: migrationClasses,\n889\t        entities: entityClasses,\n890\t        synchronize: false,\n891\t        migrationsRun: false,\n892\t        // Migration table inside the service schema; the production\n893\t        // MigrationRunnerService uses the same convention.\n894\t        migrationsTableName: 'migrations',\n895\t        // Pin search_path on every connection acquired from this pool.\n896\t        // Why: TypeORM's built-in MigrationExecutor (invoked by\n897\t        // ds.runMigrations() below) does NOT pin search_path —\n898\t        // production MigrationRunnerService does, but this test bypasses\n899\t        // the runner and goes straight to the executor. Migrations that\n900\t        // use unqualified table references (e.g. `ALTER TABLE \"users\"`,\n901\t        // `FROM \"users\"`) then resolve against the connection's default\n902\t        // search_path (`public`) and fail with `relation \"users\" does\n903\t        // not exist` on a fresh DB where auth tables only live in the\n904\t        // auth schema. The libpq `-c search_path=...` option sets the\n905\t        // path at session-start time, before any migration SQL runs,\n906\t        // and survives across the BEGIN/COMMIT cycles MigrationExecutor\n907\t        // uses in `transaction: 'each'` mode.\n908\t        extra: {\n909\t          options: `-c search_path=\"${svc.schema}\",public`,\n910\t        },\n911\t      });\n912\t\n913\t      try {\n914\t        await ds.initialize();\n915\t        // Skip runMigrations() for services with empty migration sets —\n916\t        // gateway-api owns no migrations dir and config-service ships\n917\t        // only `.gitkeep`. Calling runMigrations() with an empty list is\n918\t        // a no-op in TypeORM, but skipping the call avoids creating the\n919\t        // empty `<schema>.migrations` ledger table that would skew the\n920\t        // \"ledger is non-empty\" assertion below.\n921\t        if (migrationClasses.length > 0) {\n922\t          await runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority(() => ds.runMigrations({ transaction: 'each' }));\n923\t        }\n924\t      } catch (err) {\n925\t        // Surface which service failed so the diagnostic message is\n926\t        // actionable from CI logs. Do not swallow — re-throw so Jest\n927\t        // marks the suite as failed.\n928\t        throw new Error(\n929\t          `[bootstrap-from-scratch] Migration run failed for service \"${svc.name}\" ` +\n930\t            `(schema=${svc.schema}, dir=${svc.migrationsDir}): ${(err as Error).message}\\n` +\n931\t            `${(err as Error).stack ?? ''}`,\n932\t        );\n933\t      }\n934\t      dataSources.set(svc.name, ds);\n935\t    }\n936\t  }, 600_000);\n937\t\n938\t  afterAll(async () => {\n939\t    for (const ds of dataSources.values()) {\n940\t      if (ds.isInitialized) {\n941\t        await ds.destroy().catch(() => {\n942\t          /* swallow — teardown */\n943\t        });\n944\t      }\n945\t    }\n946\t    if (postgresContainer) {\n947\t      await postgresContainer.stop({ remove: true }).catch(() => {\n948\t        /* swallow — teardown */\n949\t      });\n950\t    }\n951\t  });\n952\t\n953\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n954\t  // Assertions: every check below is a single-table-or-column SQL probe\n955\t  // against the ground-truth `information_schema` views. No app code,\n956\t  // no GraphQL, no ORM — just \"did the SQL state we expected get\n957\t  // built?\".\n958\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n959\t\n960\t  // Helper: pick any initialized DataSource to run schema-introspection\n961\t  // queries. Schema visibility is global within the database, so any\n962\t  // connection works.\n963\t  function probeDs(): DataSource {\n964\t    const ds = dataSources.get('auth-service');\n965\t    if (!ds || !ds.isInitialized) {\n966\t      throw new Error('auth-service DataSource not initialized — beforeAll did not complete.');\n967\t    }\n968\t    return ds;\n969\t  }\n970\t\n971\t  it('init scripts created all 15 service schemas', async () => {\n972\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ schema_name: string }[]>(\n973\t      `SELECT schema_name FROM information_schema.schemata\n974\t       WHERE schema_name = ANY($1::text[])\n975\t       ORDER BY schema_name`,\n976\t      [REQUIRED_SCHEMAS],\n977\t    );\n978\t    const found = new Set(rows.map((r) => r.schema_name));\n979\t    const missing = REQUIRED_SCHEMAS.filter((s) => !found.has(s));\n980\t    if (missing.length > 0) {\n981\t      throw new Error(\n982\t        `Init scripts did not create expected schemas: missing ${missing.join(', ')}. ` +\n983\t          `Verify infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/00-init-schemas.sh has a CREATE SCHEMA ` +\n984\t          `entry for each. Required total: ${REQUIRED_SCHEMAS.length}.`,\n985\t      );\n986\t    }\n987\t  });\n988\t\n989\t  it.each(CRITICAL_AUTH_USERS_COLUMNS)(\n990\t    'auth.users has critical column \"%s\" (regression class: squash drop)',\n991\t    async (column) => {\n992\t      const rows = await probeDs().query<{ column_name: string }[]>(\n993\t        `SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns\n994\t         WHERE table_schema = 'auth' AND table_name = 'users' AND column_name = $1`,\n995\t        [column],\n996\t      );\n997\t      if (rows.length === 0) {\n998\t        throw new Error(\n999\t          `auth.users.${column} is missing after fresh-volume init. ` +\n1000\t            `This is the exact failure class fixed by ` +\n1001\t            `apps/auth-service/src/migrations/1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema.ts. ` +\n1002\t            `Verify that migration is present in the auth-service migrations directory ` +\n1003\t            `and creates this column idempotently.`,\n1004\t        );\n1005\t      }\n1006\t    },\n1007\t  );\n1008\t\n1009\t  it.each(AUTH_BASELINE_TABLES)('auth.%s table exists after migration chain', async (table) => {\n1010\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1011\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1012\t       WHERE table_schema = 'auth' AND table_name = $1`,\n1013\t      [table],\n1014\t    );\n1015\t    if (rows.length === 0) {\n1016\t      throw new Error(\n1017\t        `auth.${table} is missing — this is one of the 12 baseline tables ` +\n1018\t          `restored by 1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema. If a future migration ` +\n1019\t          `legitimately drops it, update AUTH_BASELINE_TABLES in this spec.`,\n1020\t      );\n1021\t    }\n1022\t  });\n1023\t\n1024\t  it('farm.batches_v2 exists (entity-anchored table created in migration chain)', async () => {\n1025\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1026\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1027\t       WHERE table_schema = 'farm' AND table_name = 'batches_v2'`,\n1028\t    );\n1029\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1030\t  });\n1031\t\n1032\t  it('farm.farms exists (core farm-service table)', async () => {\n1033\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1034\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1035\t       WHERE table_schema = 'farm' AND table_name = 'farms'`,\n1036\t    );\n1037\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1038\t  });\n1039\t\n1040\t  it('farm.tanks exists (post-equipment migration carry-over)', async () => {\n1041\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1042\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1043\t       WHERE table_schema = 'farm' AND table_name = 'tanks'`,\n1044\t    );\n1045\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1046\t  });\n1047\t\n1048\t  it.each(EXPECTED_HYPERTABLES)(\n1049\t    '$schema.$table is a TimescaleDB hypertable',\n1050\t    async ({ schema, table }) => {\n1051\t      // Source-schema hypertable existence is the strongest signal that\n1052\t      // the create_hypertable() call inside the migration ran. It says\n1053\t      // nothing about the per-tenant copies: the runtime\n1054\t      // createSensorMetricsHypertable() path this comment used to cite is\n1055\t      // gone, tenant schemas are built by migration replay instead, and no\n1056\t      // spec provisions a tenant against a live database (DATA-CRITICAL-010).\n1057\t      const rows = await probeDs().query<{ hypertable_name: string }[]>(\n1058\t        `SELECT hypertable_name FROM timescaledb_information.hypertables\n1059\t         WHERE hypertable_schema = $1 AND hypertable_name = $2`,\n1060\t        [schema, table],\n1061\t      );\n1062\t      if (rows.length !== 1) {\n1063\t        throw new Error(\n1064\t          `${schema}.${table} is not registered as a TimescaleDB hypertable. ` +\n1065\t            `Either the timescaledb extension was not enabled (check ` +\n1066\t            `init-scripts/00-init-schemas.sh CREATE EXTENSION) or the ` +\n1067\t            `migration that calls create_hypertable() for this table did not run.`,\n1068\t        );\n1069\t      }\n1070\t    },\n1071\t  );\n1072\t\n1073\t  it('db-migrate creates tenant sensor rollups with owner and runtime read access', async () => {\n1074\t    const tenantSchema = 'tenant_aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa';\n1075\t    const db = probeDs();\n1076\t    const queryRunner = db.createQueryRunner();\n1077\t\n1078\t    await queryRunner.connect();\n1079\t    try {\n1080\t      await queryRunner.query(`DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS \"${tenantSchema}\" CASCADE`);\n1081\t      await queryRunner.query(`CREATE SCHEMA \"${tenantSchema}\"`);\n1082\t      await queryRunner.query(\n1083\t        `CREATE TABLE \"${tenantSchema}\".\"sensor_metrics\"\n1084\t         (LIKE \"sensor\".\"sensor_metrics\" INCLUDING ALL)`,\n1085\t      );\n1086\t      await queryRunner.query(\n1087\t        `SELECT create_hypertable(\n1088\t           '\"${tenantSchema}\".\"sensor_metrics\"',\n1089\t           'time',\n1090\t           if_not_exists => true\n1091\t         )`,\n1092\t      );\n1093\t\n1094\t      const result = await ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority(\n1095\t        queryRunner,\n1096\t        tenantSchema,\n1097\t      );\n1098\t      expect(result.timescalePresent).toBe(true);\n1099\t\n1100\t      const aggregateOwnerRoles = (await queryRunner.query(\n1101\t        `SELECT rolcanlogin,\n1102\t                rolpassword IS NULL AS has_no_password,\n1103\t                rolsuper,\n1104\t                rolcreatedb,\n1105\t                rolcreaterole,\n1106\t                rolreplication,\n1107\t                rolbypassrls\n1108\t           -- pg_roles masks rolpassword as ********, including passwordless\n1109\t           -- roles. This bootstrap probe runs as the database superuser, so\n1110\t           -- pg_authid is the authoritative source for the NULL assertion.\n1111\t           FROM pg_catalog.pg_authid\n1112\t          WHERE rolname = 'sensor_aggregate_owner'`,\n1113\t      )) as Array<{\n1114\t        has_no_password: boolean;\n1115\t        rolbypassrls: boolean;\n1116\t        rolcanlogin: boolean;\n1117\t        rolcreatedb: boolean;\n1118\t        rolcreaterole: boolean;\n1119\t        rolreplication: boolean;\n1120\t        rolsuper: boolean;\n1121\t      }>;\n1122\t      expect(aggregateOwnerRoles).toEqual([\n1123\t        {\n1124\t          has_no_password: true,\n1125\t          rolbypassrls: false,\n1126\t          rolcanlogin: true,\n1127\t          rolcreatedb: false,\n1128\t          rolcreaterole: false,\n1129\t          rolreplication: false,\n1130\t          rolsuper: false,\n1131\t        },\n1132\t      ]);\n1133\t\n1134\t      const aggregates = (await queryRunner.query(\n1135\t        `SELECT view_name,\n1136\t                view_owner,\n1137\t                has_table_privilege(\n1138\t                  'sensor_service',\n1139\t                  format('%I.%I', view_schema, view_name),\n1140\t                  'SELECT'\n1141\t                ) AS runtime_can_select\n1142\t           FROM timescaledb_information.continuous_aggregates\n1143\t          WHERE view_schema = $1\n1144\t          ORDER BY view_name`,\n1145\t        [tenantSchema],\n1146\t      )) as Array<{ view_name: string; view_owner: string; runtime_can_select: boolean }>;\n1147\t\n1148\t      expect(aggregates).toEqual([\n1149\t        {\n1150\t          view_name: 'metrics_1day',\n1151\t          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n1152\t          runtime_can_select: true,\n1153\t        },\n1154\t        {\n1155\t          view_name: 'metrics_1hour',\n1156\t          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n1157\t          runtime_can_select: true,\n1158\t        },\n1159\t        {\n1160\t          view_name: 'metrics_1min',\n1161\t          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n1162\t          runtime_can_select: true,\n1163\t        },\n1164\t      ]);\n1165\t    } finally {\n1166\t      try {\n1167\t        await queryRunner.query(`DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS \"${tenantSchema}\" CASCADE`);\n1168\t      } finally {\n1169\t        await queryRunner.release();\n1170\t      }\n1171\t    }\n1172\t  });\n1173\t\n1174\t  it('billing.plans exists (squash-restoration target)', async () => {\n1175\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1176\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1177\t       WHERE table_schema = 'billing' AND table_name = 'plans'`,\n1178\t    );\n1179\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1180\t  });\n1181\t\n1182\t  it('billing.payments exists (squash-restoration target)', async () => {\n1183\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1184\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1185\t       WHERE table_schema = 'billing' AND table_name = 'payments'`,\n1186\t    );\n1187\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1188\t  });\n1189\t\n1190\t  it('alert.alert_rules exists (alert-engine baseline)', async () => {\n1191\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1192\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1193\t       WHERE table_schema = 'alert' AND table_name = 'alert_rules'`,\n1194\t    );\n1195\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1196\t  });\n1197\t\n1198\t  it('admin.audit_logs exists (created by 11-service-audit-tables.sql)', async () => {\n1199\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1200\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1201\t       WHERE table_schema = 'admin' AND table_name = 'audit_logs'`,\n1202\t    );\n1203\t    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n1204\t  });\n1205\t\n1206\t  it('shared schema contains the 4 cross-service tables (audit_logs, gdpr_data_requests, user_consents, access_logs)', async () => {\n1207\t    // user_permissions retired 2026-07-12 (ADR-042, ORPHAN-HIGH-378):\n1208\t    // the canonical shared set is audit_logs, gdpr_data_requests,\n1209\t    // user_consents, access_logs.\n1210\t    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n1211\t      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n1212\t       WHERE table_schema = 'shared' AND table_name = ANY($1::text[])`,\n1213\t      [['audit_logs', 'gdpr_data_requests', 'user_consents', 'access_logs']],\n1214\t    );\n1215\t    const found = new Set(rows.map((r) => r.table_name));\n1216\t    for (const t of ['audit_logs', 'gdpr_data_requests', 'user_consents', 'access_logs']) {\n1217\t      if (!found.has(t)) {\n1218\t        throw new Error(\n1219\t          `shared.${t} missing after platform bootstrap. Check ` +\n1220\t            `apps/db-migrate/src/sql/platform-bootstrap/006-shared-schema-tables.sql.`,\n1221\t        );\n1222\t      }\n1223\t    }\n1224\t  });\n1225\t\n1226\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1227\t  // Part A.3 — Migration ledger completeness.\n1228\t  //\n1229\t  // Failure mode this catches: TypeORM \"thought it ran\" but skipped\n1230\t  // migrations. Empirically observed when the migrations array passed\n1231\t  // to TypeOrmModule.forRoot was a stale glob that resolved to fewer\n1232\t  // files than the migrations directory holds, and runMigrations()\n1233\t  // returned without error because every entry it knew about was\n1234\t  // already applied. The ledger row count then drifts below the\n1235\t  // file-system count and silent under-application goes undetected\n1236\t  // until a fresh deploy reveals missing tables.\n1237\t  //\n1238\t  // Assertion: count(<schema>.migrations rows) == count(migration files\n1239\t  // on disk that match the timestamp+PascalCase regex). Skip services\n1240\t  // with empty migration sets (gateway-api, config-service: those have\n1241\t  // NO ledger because we skipped runMigrations() entirely).\n1242\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1243\t  it.each(\n1244\t    SERVICES.filter(\n1245\t      (s) => loadMigrationClassesFromDir(join(REPO_ROOT, s.migrationsDir)).length > 0,\n1246\t    ),\n1247\t  )(\n1248\t    'service \"$name\" migration ledger row count matches on-disk migration file count',\n1249\t    async ({ name, schema, migrationsDir }) => {\n1250\t      const ds = dataSources.get(name);\n1251\t      if (!ds || !ds.isInitialized) {\n1252\t        throw new Error(`${name} DataSource not initialized.`);\n1253\t      }\n1254\t      const onDiskCount = loadMigrationClassesFromDir(join(REPO_ROOT, migrationsDir)).length;\n1255\t      const rows = await ds.query<{ count: string }[]>(\n1256\t        `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM \"${schema}\".migrations`,\n1257\t      );\n1258\t      const ledgerCount = parseInt(rows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n1259\t      if (ledgerCount !== onDiskCount) {\n1260\t        throw new Error(\n1261\t          `${schema}.migrations ledger row count drift: on-disk migrations dir has ` +\n1262\t            `${onDiskCount} timestamped files, ledger has ${ledgerCount} rows. ` +\n1263\t            `Either runMigrations() silently skipped some files (TypeORM cache or ` +\n1264\t            `metadata-loader bug) or a file landed on disk without being included ` +\n1265\t            `in the per-service migrations[] manifest.`,\n1266\t        );\n1267\t      }\n1268\t    },\n1269\t  );\n1270\t\n1271\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1272\t  // Part A.1 — Entity-surface coverage matrix.\n1273\t  //\n1274\t  // For each service that owns entities, walk every `*.entity.ts` file,\n1275\t  // load its TypeORM metadata via getMetadataArgsStorage / a metadata-\n1276\t  // only DataSource, then probe (a) table existence, (b) column\n1277\t  // existence + data type, (c) FK count, (d) index count, (e) tenant-\n1278\t  // clone RLS. The helper assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb() returns a\n1279\t  // per-entity drift array; we surface each drift entry as its own\n1280\t  // assertion failure so the CI annotation points at the exact\n1281\t  // schema.table.column pair that drifted.\n1282\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1283\t  it.each(SERVICES.filter((s) => s.entitiesGlob !== undefined))(\n1284\t    'service \"$name\" entity surface aligns with physical DB schema',\n1285\t    async ({ name, entitiesGlob }) => {\n1286\t      // entitiesGlob is set on every filtered entry — TypeScript narrows\n1287\t      // via the filter predicate, but we re-assert defensively.\n1288\t      if (entitiesGlob === undefined) {\n1289\t        throw new Error(`assert: entitiesGlob undefined for \"${name}\"`);\n1290\t      }\n1291\t      const entityClasses = loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, entitiesGlob));\n1292\t      if (entityClasses.length === 0) {\n1293\t        // A service listed with `entitiesGlob` set but zero entities\n1294\t        // discovered is a manifest bug — the glob points at the wrong\n1295\t        // directory or all entities were deleted.\n1296\t        throw new Error(\n1297\t          `service \"${name}\" declared entitiesGlob=\"${entitiesGlob}\" but ` +\n1298\t            `loadEntityClasses() found ZERO @Entity files. Update the manifest ` +\n1299\t            `(remove entitiesGlob if the service truly owns no entities) or ` +\n1300\t            `fix the path.`,\n1301\t        );\n1302\t      }\n1303\t\n1304\t      const conn: PgConnInfo = {\n1305\t        host: postgresContainer.getHost(),\n1306\t        port: postgresContainer.getMappedPort(5432),\n1307\t        username: DATABASE_USER,\n1308\t        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n1309\t        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n1310\t      };\n1311\t      const results = await assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb(name, entityClasses, conn);\n1312\t\n1313\t      const drifts = results.flatMap((r) => r.drift.map((d) => `  ${r.schema}.${r.table}: ${d}`));\n1314\t      if (drifts.length > 0) {\n1315\t        throw new Error(\n1316\t          `Entity surface drift in service \"${name}\" (${drifts.length} issue(s)):\\n` +\n1317\t            drifts.join('\\n'),\n1318\t        );\n1319\t      }\n1320\t    },\n1321\t    // Per-service entity-surface scan can iterate dozens of entities;\n1322\t    // give it generous headroom but still a hard ceiling so a hung\n1323\t    // metadata-DataSource teardown does not pin the worker.\n1324\t    120_000,\n1325\t  );\n1326\t\n1327\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1328\t  // Part A.4 — Drift validator runtime gate.\n1329\t  //\n1330\t  // After migrations run, instantiate the SchemaDriftValidator factory\n1331\t  // for each entity-owning service with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true. The\n1332\t  // validator's onApplicationBootstrap() hook walks the same\n1333\t  // EntityMetadata graph the runtime uses and throws on any of the\n1334\t  // drift classes registered in drift-classes.ts. If the bootstrap-\n1335\t  // from-scratch pipeline produces a clean DB, this gate MUST pass —\n1336\t  // failure here means a service would crash on cold start in\n1337\t  // staging/production with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true (the production\n1338\t  // posture per ADR-012).\n1339\t  //\n1340\t  // We instantiate the validator class directly with a per-service\n1341\t  // metadata-only DataSource and a lightweight ConfigService stub\n1342\t  // rather than booting a full NestJS app — same observable\n1343\t  // behaviour, ~30s saved per service.\n1344\t  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n1345\t  it.each(SERVICES.filter((s) => s.entitiesGlob !== undefined))(\n1346\t    'service \"$name\" passes SchemaDriftValidator with FATAL=true after migrations',\n1347\t    async ({ name, schema, entitiesGlob }) => {\n1348\t      if (entitiesGlob === undefined) {\n1349\t        throw new Error(`assert: entitiesGlob undefined for \"${name}\"`);\n1350\t      }\n1351\t      const entityClasses = loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, entitiesGlob));\n1352\t\n1353\t      // Per-service metadata DataSource so EntityMetadata graphs match\n1354\t      // the validator's expected shape. The validator scans\n1355\t      // dataSource.entityMetadatas, NOT the runtime DB — entity loading\n1356\t      // is the only ceremony required.\n1357\t      const driftDs = new DataSource({\n1358\t        type: 'postgres',\n1359\t        host: postgresContainer.getHost(),\n1360\t        port: postgresContainer.getMappedPort(5432),\n1361\t        username: DATABASE_USER,\n1362\t        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n1363\t        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n1364\t        schema,\n1365\t        entities: [...entityClasses],\n1366\t        synchronize: false,\n1367\t        migrationsRun: false,\n1368\t      });\n1369\t      await driftDs.initialize();\n1370\t\n1371\t      // Real ConfigService seeded with only the keys the validator reads —\n1372\t      // a genuine instance avoids any cast and exercises the same\n1373\t      // get(key, default) resolution the runtime uses. SCHEMA_DRIFT_ENABLED +\n1374\t      // FATAL drive the gate; AQUA_ENV / NODE_ENV feed the emergency-override\n1375\t      // lookup which short-circuits when no override row exists.\n1376\t      const configStub = new ConfigService({\n1377\t        SCHEMA_DRIFT_ENABLED: 'true',\n1378\t        SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL: 'true',\n1379\t        SCHEMA_DRIFT_TENANT_SCAN_ENABLED: 'false',\n1380\t        AQUA_ENV: 'test',\n1381\t        NODE_ENV: 'test',\n1382\t      });\n1383\t\n1384\t      const factory = loadDriftValidatorFactory();\n1385\t      const ValidatorClass = factory(\n1386\t        // Validator names must match /^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$/ — the\n1387\t        // service manifest names already comply.\n1388\t        name,\n1389\t      );\n1390\t      const validator = new ValidatorClass(driftDs, configStub);\n1391\t\n1392\t      try {\n1393\t        await validator.onApplicationBootstrap();\n1394\t      } catch (err) {\n1395\t        const message = (err as Error).message;\n1396\t        // Surface the FIRST drift class so a CI reader sees the exact\n1397\t        // failure mode (location vs type vs nullability) without\n1398\t        // scrolling. The validator already prefixes its throw with\n1399\t        // \"Schema drift detected in N place(s)\" — keep that intact.\n1400\t        throw new Error(\n1401\t          `[Part A.4] SchemaDriftValidator threw for service \"${name}\" after ` +\n1402\t            `bootstrap-from-scratch migrations completed. This means the ` +\n1403\t            `migration chain produced a DB shape that the entity layer ` +\n1404\t            `disagrees with — a service running with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true ` +\n1405\t            `would refuse to boot.\\n\\n${message}`,\n1406\t        );\n1407\t      } finally {\n1408\t        await driftDs.destroy().catch(() => {\n1409\t          /* swallow — teardown */\n1410\t        });\n1411\t      }\n1412\t    },\n1413\t    180_000,\n1414\t  );\n1415\t});\n1416\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"90272c0c-5aaa-42c6-b4bc-529805e57f54","uuid":"a1d89dec-7ebd-4c02-88f8-25771a2c41ab","timestamp":"2026-10-08T04:26:43.669Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-310dc6fc293d/apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts","content":"/**\n * Bootstrap From Scratch\n * ============================================================================\n *\n * Proves that the fresh-volume init pipeline — `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/*`\n * + every service's TypeORM migration chain — produces a clean, complete\n * platform schema when run against an empty Postgres.\n *\n * # Why this test exists\n *\n * The codebase's bootstrap pipeline silently broke when several\n * `CREATE TABLE` migrations were squashed out of source. Production survived\n * because nobody did a fresh-volume init for months — every existing\n * environment had the squashed-out tables already on disk. The first\n * fresh-volume deploy crashed at `1781100000000-ConvertTimestampToTimestamptz`\n * trying to ALTER `auth.users.mfaLockedUntil` (a column whose CREATE was\n * gone). That is the exact regression class this invariant guards against.\n *\n * # What it does\n *\n *   1. Spin up `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` (the production image, by\n *      digest pinned to docker-compose.infra.yml).\n *   2. Copy `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/` into\n *      `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` — Postgres runs every script in\n *      lexical order on first boot of an empty PGDATA volume.\n *   3. Connect, then for every service that owns a TypeORM migration\n *      directory, dynamically load every migration class and run them\n *      through a per-service DataSource bound to that service's schema.\n *   4. Assert schema-level invariants: schemas exist, the previously\n *      missing baseline tables now exist, the previously missing\n *      `auth.users` columns now exist, the sensor hypertable was\n *      created, db-migrate can owner-align a real tenant's continuous\n *      aggregates, and each service's migration ledger is non-empty\n *      (proving the migrations actually ran).\n *\n * # When this test fails\n *\n *   - A migration that creates a baseline table was squashed →\n *     restore the CREATE in the earliest service migration. (This is\n *     the original regression class.)\n *   - An init-script SQL file was deleted without an equivalent\n *     migration → either restore the script OR write a migration that\n *     creates the same tables idempotently.\n *   - A new entity was added without a corresponding CREATE TABLE\n *     migration AND without an init-script entry → add the migration.\n *   - The init scripts assume a privilege the bootstrap POSTGRES_USER\n *     does not have on a fresh container → fix the script to use\n *     a less-privileged path or supply additional bootstrap material.\n *\n * # Why testcontainers (not the shared TestDatabase pool)\n *\n * The other e2e/integration specs assert state of an *already-running*\n * database that the dev/CI infra brought up — they're checking\n * application-level behaviour. This test asserts the bootstrap ITSELF\n * works on an EMPTY volume. It MUST start with no PGDATA, run the init\n * scripts, then run the migration chain. testcontainers is the only\n * way to get that hermetic guarantee inside a Jest worker.\n *\n * # Runtime\n *\n * ~3-5 minutes on a cold runner: image pull + init-script execution +\n * 100+ migrations across 13 services. CI gate runs only on PRs that\n * touch `apps/*\\/src/**\\/migrations/**` or\n * `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/**` (see CI workflow\n * `.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml` integration; if the gate\n * is not yet wired, `npm run test:bootstrap` is the local entry point).\n */\n\nimport { readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from 'fs';\nimport { createRequire } from 'module';\nimport { join, resolve } from 'path';\n\nimport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\nimport { GenericContainer, type StartedTestContainer, Wait } from 'testcontainers';\nimport { DataSource, type MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n\n// ADR-031 Platform Bootstrap Atom — runs the schema/role/extension/function/\n// shared-table DDL contract that init-scripts USED to own pre-cutover. The\n// init-scripts directory copied below now contains only `01-init-databases.sql`\n// (initdb-only DB GRANTs). Schemas, roles, functions, and shared.* tables\n// are created by the call to runPlatformBootstrap() below before any\n// per-service migration loop runs.\nimport {\n  runPlatformBootstrap,\n  resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir,\n} from '../platform-bootstrap.service';\nimport { ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority } from '../tenant-sensor-continuous-aggregate-authority';\n\n// Constructor type for the @Entity / migration classes this spec loads\n// dynamically. TypeORM's DataSource `entities`/`migrations` options accept\n// `Function`, but `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-function-type` (correctly)\n// rejects the bare `Function` type; a precise construct signature is both\n// assignable to TypeORM's `Function` slot AND keeps the collected values\n// typed as classes rather than arbitrary callables.\ntype EntityClass = new (...args: never[]) => object;\n\n// Synchronous module loader bound to this spec's location. `import`/`await\n// import()` cannot be used here: loadMigrationClassesFromDir is invoked inside\n// `it.each(...)` arguments that Jest evaluates synchronously at collection\n// time, so the load must be synchronous. `createRequire(__filename)` resolves\n// migration classes and the cross-package schema-drift-validator through\n// ts-jest at runtime, so the static type-check graph never reaches across\n// project rootDirs — the same dynamic-load pattern the db-migrate\n// platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts relies on.\nconst requireModule = createRequire(__filename);\n\n// Structured logger for the (rare) entity-load skip path — `console.*` is\n// banned platform-wide; the NestJS Logger keeps the diagnostic on the same\n// structured-logging path the runtime services use.\nconst logger = new Logger('bootstrap-from-scratch');\n\n// Local narrowing helper. db-migrate has @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion\n// ENABLED (unlike the e2e project, whose assertDefined SSoT this replaces), so a\n// bare `!` is banned here. assertDefined narrows `T | undefined` to `T` and fails\n// loudly at the assertion site instead of surfacing as a cryptic\n// \"cannot read properties of undefined\" further downstream.\nfunction assertDefined<T>(value: T | null | undefined, message?: string): T {\n  if (value === null || value === undefined) {\n    throw new Error(message ?? 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');\n  }\n  return value;\n}\n\n// Dynamic load of the backend-common schema-drift-validator. Importing\n// schema-drift-validator.service.ts directly via `import { ... }` would pull a\n// file from another Nx project into db-migrate's type-check graph. ts-jest\n// resolves this lazily at runtime (same pattern the migration loader uses for\n// migration classes), so the validator's stable runtime contract is exercised\n// without a static cross-project import. From this spec's location\n// (apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/) the repo root is four levels up.\ntype SchemaDriftValidatorFactory = (\n  serviceName: string,\n) => new (ds: DataSource, cs: ConfigService) => { onApplicationBootstrap(): Promise<void> };\nfunction loadDriftValidatorFactory(): SchemaDriftValidatorFactory {\n  const mod = requireModule(\n    '../../../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-drift-validator.service',\n  ) as {\n    createSchemaDriftValidator: SchemaDriftValidatorFactory;\n  };\n  return mod.createSchemaDriftValidator;\n}\n\n// The same image+digest the production stack uses — see\n// docker-compose.infra.yml line 12. Pinning by digest guarantees the\n// init-script behaviour we're proving here matches what runs in\n// production. Bumping the digest is a deliberate review surface.\nconst POSTGRES_IMAGE =\n  'timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7';\n\nconst DATABASE_NAME = 'aquaculture';\nconst DATABASE_USER = 'aquaculture';\nconst DATABASE_PASSWORD = 'aquaculture-test';\nconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\nconst SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS = [\n  'AUTH_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'FARM_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'SENSOR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'BILLING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'HR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'ALERT_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'ADMIN_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'GATEWAY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'HYDROPONICS_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'AI_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'MESSAGING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'OBSERVABILITY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'EVENT_STORE_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n  'CONFIG_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n] as const;\n\n// Repo root, derived from this file's location:\n//   apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts -> ../../../..\n// (mirrors how the sibling platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts resolves it).\nconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..');\nconst INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR = join(REPO_ROOT, 'infrastructure', 'docker', 'init-scripts');\n\nasync function runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n  const previous = process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV];\n  process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = '1';\n  try {\n    return await operation();\n  } finally {\n    if (previous === undefined) {\n      Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env, DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV);\n    } else {\n      process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = previous;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Per-service migration manifest.\n *\n * Each entry maps a service to:\n *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n *     when adding a service.\n *\n * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n * else is alphabetical.\n */\ninterface ServiceManifest {\n  name: string;\n  schema: string;\n  migrationsDir: string;\n  /**\n   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n   * exists in the DB. Services without entities (gateway-api) leave it\n   * unset.\n   */\n  entitiesGlob?: string;\n}\n\nconst SERVICES: ServiceManifest[] = [\n  // auth FIRST — every other service's FKs reference auth.tenants.\n  {\n    name: 'auth-service',\n    schema: 'auth',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/auth-service/src',\n  },\n  // Then the rest, ordered to minimize cross-schema FK ordering hazards.\n  {\n    name: 'farm-service',\n    schema: 'farm',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/farm-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'sensor-service',\n    schema: 'sensor',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/sensor-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'hr-service',\n    schema: 'hr',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hr-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'messaging-service',\n    schema: 'messaging',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/messaging-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'alert-engine',\n    schema: 'alert',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/alert-engine/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'billing-service',\n    schema: 'billing',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/billing-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/billing-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'notification-service',\n    schema: 'notification',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/notification-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/notification-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'observability-service',\n    schema: 'observability',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/observability-service/src/database/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/observability-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'admin-api-service',\n    schema: 'admin',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/admin-api-service/src',\n  },\n  {\n    name: 'event-store-service',\n    schema: 'event_store',\n    migrationsDir: 'apps/event-store-service/src/migrations',\n    entitiesGlob: 'apps/event-store-service/src',\n  },\n  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — close the manifest gap. The 4 services\n  // below were absent from the original 11-service manifest, so the\n  // bootstrap test passed even when their migration chains were empty\n  // or their entity-surface drifted from the on-disk schema. Adding\n  // them here closes the regression class:\n  //\n  //   hydroponics-service / ai-service — schema-per-tenant services\n  //     with their own baseline migrations + entity surface.\n  //   config-service — platform-level service. Migrations dir is\n  //     intentionally empty (.gitkeep only) — the entity-surface\n  //     check still runs, the migration runner is a no-op.\n  //   gateway-api — pure HTTP fronting, owns NO entities and NO\n  //     migrations.
